# Israeli occupation of the West Bank

|  | This article **may be [too long](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Article_size) to read and navigate comfortably**. Consider [splitting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Splitting) content into sub-articles, [condensing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Summary_style) it, or adding [subheadings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Section#Subsections). Please discuss this issue on the article's [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Israeli_occupation_of_the_West_Bank). *(September 2025)* |
| --- | --- |



Map of West Bank settlements and closures in January 2006:&#x20;

&#x20; Closed military areas or [settlement](15123.md) boundary areas or areas isolated by the Israeli [West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_barrier)

&#x20; Settlements, outposts or military bases

&#x20; Route of the Barrier

The [West Bank](33209.md), including [East Jerusalem](343508.md), has been under [military occupation](816214.md) by [Israel](9282173.md) since 7 June 1967, when Israeli forces captured the territory, then [occupied by Jordan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank), during the [Six-Day War](29328.md).[^cite_note-1] The West Bank is part of the state of [Palestine](241405.md), and its status as a militarily occupied territory has been affirmed by the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) (ICJ) and, [with the exception of East Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Status_of_territories_occupied_by_Israel_in_1967), by the [Israeli Supreme Court](1666510.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDomb2007511–513-2] The West Bank, excepting East Jerusalem, is administered by the [Israeli Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration), a branch of the [Israeli Ministry of Defense](3226446.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenisti2012208–209-3][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKimmerling200378,_n.17-4][^cite_note-7] Considered to be a classic example of an "[intractable conflict](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intractable_conflict)",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBar-TalAlon2017317-8][^cite_note-9] Israel's occupation is now the [longest in modern history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_military_occupations#Contemporary_occupations).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERoberts199044-10][^cite_note-11][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKarayanni2014xv-12][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHajjar20052-13] Though [its occupation is illegal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legal_Consequences_of_the_Construction_of_a_Wall_in_the_Occupied_Palestinian_Territory),[^cite_note-14] Israel has cited several reasons for retaining the West Bank within its ambit: historic rights stemming from the [Balfour Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balfour_Declaration); security grounds, both internal and external; and the area's symbolic value for Jews.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERathbun2014205-15]

Israel has controversially, and in [contravention of international law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements), established numerous [Jewish settlements](15123.md) throughout the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKumaraswamy2015409-16] The [United Nations Security Council](31956.md) has repeatedly affirmed that settlements in that territory are a "flagrant violation of international law", most recently in 2016 with [United Nations Security Council Resolution 2334](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Security_Council_Resolution_2334).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUNSC2016-17] The ICJ has also found that the establishment of Israeli settlements is illegal under international law.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBeaumont2024-18] The creation and ongoing expansion of the settlements have led to Israel's policies being criticized as [an example of settler colonialism](70418136.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReuveny2008325–374-19][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShafir1984803-20][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELentin201855-21][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHandel2014505-22][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZureik201551-23][^cite_note-29]

[Israel has been accused of major violations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_violations_against_Palestinians_by_Israel) of [international human rights law](219877.md), including [collective punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collective_punishment), in its administration of the [occupied Palestinian territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_occupied_territories#Palestinian_territories).[^cite_note-30] Israeli settlers and civilians living or traveling through the West Bank [are subject to Israeli law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_law_in_the_West_Bank_settlements), and are represented in the [Knesset](74656.md); in contrast, Palestinian civilians, mostly confined to [scattered enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_enclaves), are subject to martial law and are not permitted to vote in Israel's national elections.[^cite_note-31] This two-tiered system has caused [Israel to be accused of committing apartheid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_and_apartheid), a charge that Israel rejects entirely.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZureik201577–78-32][^cite_note-33][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdamMoodley200519ff,_59ff-34][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChappell2022-35][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHolmes2021-36] Israel's vast military superiority, with a modern army and air force, compared to the Palestinian use of guerrilla tactics, has led to accusations of war crimes on both sides, with Israel being accused of disproportionality and the Palestinians accused of indiscriminate attacks.

The occupation also has numerous critics within Israel itself, with some Israeli conscripts [refusing to serve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refusal_to_serve_in_the_IDF) due to their objections to the occupation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKidron201318-37] The legal status of the occupation itself, and not just the actions taken as a part of it, have been increasingly scrutinized by the international community and by scholars in the field of international law, with most finding that regardless of whether the occupation had been legal when it began, it has become illegal over time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEErakat2019-38][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFinkelstein2018-39][^cite_note-40]

## The West Bank in 1967

Further information: [Jordanian annexation of the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank)

[Hebron](38577.md)

[Nablus](233369.md)

[Jenin](50997.md)

Relief map of the West Bank

Israel's economy was 10 times larger than the West Bank's on the eve of the occupation, but had experienced two years of recession. The West Bank's population stood between 585,500 and 803,600 and, while under [Jordanian rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordanian_annexation_of_the_West_Bank), accounted for 40% of Jordan's GNP,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMansour201571,_73–74-41] with an annual growth rate of 6–8%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETumaDarin-Drabkin197847,_50-42] Ownership of land was generally collective, and the 19th century [Ottoman land code](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Land_Code_of_1858) prevailed, which classified land as either – *[waqf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waqf)*, *[mülk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/M%C3%BClk)*, *miri*, *matruke*, and *mawat* – the last three being formally state land, though Jordan never considered these last three as state property, and only a very small proportion of the West Bank was registered as such under Jordanian rule.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShehadeh1985b47-43]

Education was (and remains[^cite_note-44]) a high priority,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDavies197965-45] The enrollment rate averaged an annual increase of 7% over the prior decade, and by 1966, Palestinian youth had the highest enrollment rate of all Arab countries.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETumaDarin-Drabkin197848–49-46] Palestinians in the West Bank had a favourable educational basis compared to [Israeli Arabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Arabs) and Jordanian youth, due to the preexisting provisions of the Jordanian school system which provided 12 years of free and compulsory education, with some 44.6% of West Bank teenagers in the 15–17 age group participating in some form of secondary schooling.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETumaDarin-Drabkin197848–49-46]

### Conquest

Main article: [Six-Day War](29328.md)

In 1956, the Israeli leader [David Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion) stated that: "Jordan has no right to exist. \[...] The territory to the West of the Jordan should be made an autonomous region of Israel".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESlater1994185-47] There had been a very strong opposition to any "Balkanization" or division of Palestine, especially among American Zionists, in the mid-late thirties, since it would have made a prospective homeland, thus truncated, suicidally small.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerman199251–67-48] It was in this context that [Ben-Gurion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ben-Gurion) argued forcefully for accepting [partition agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peel_Commission) as temporary measures, steps on the way to an incremental incorporation of all of Palestine into a Jewish state.[^cite_note-49]

According to Israeli historian Adam Raz, as early as 1961, the [IDF](39237.md) had drawn up meticulous plans for the conquest and retention of not only the West Bank, but also the [Sinai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinai_Peninsula) and [Gaza Strip](12047.md) from Egypt, and the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) from Syria. In August 1963, within the framework of "expected directions of expansion", [southern Lebanon](4205968.md) up to the [Litani River](1920925.md) was also included. Though international pressure might foreseeably force Israel to evacuate these conquered lands, contingency plans also envisaged political circumstances whose development would enable Israel to maintain control of these occupied territory indefinitely. The model for controlling Palestinians in the West Bank, were this to eventuate, was to be modeled on the Israeli governance of their Palestinian communities under a strict regime of permits.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERaz2021a-50]

Before the [Six-Day War](29328.md), there had been an unwritten agreement between Israel and the Jordanian government to uphold the neutrality of the border between the two countries along the [Green Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Green_Line_\(Israel\)). According to [King Hussein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hussein_of_Jordan), after Israel retaliated against Syrian-backed guerrilla infiltrations and sabotage[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParker1992178-51] by conducting on 13 November 1966 [an assault on Samu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samu_incident) in the [West Bank](33209.md), an area administered by [Jordan](7515964.md), that tacit accord was broken.[^cite_note-52] After Israel attacked Egypt at 8 a.m. on 5 June 1967, Jordan responded by shelling Israeli targets in [West Jerusalem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Jerusalem), and settlements along the border and then, after ignoring an Israeli warning, by attacking Israeli airfields in [Ramat David](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramat_David) and [Kfar Syrkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kfar_Syrkin), but also [Netanya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netanya).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShlaim201244–45-53] In response, the Israeli army in a swift campaign took possession of East Jerusalem and, after news that King Hussein had ordered his forces to withdraw across the Jordan, took the entire West Bank by noon on 8 June.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEParker1992180-54][^cite_note-55]

Israel [expelled many people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Palestinian_exodus) from areas it had conquered, beginning with an estimated 12,000 people who on the very first day were rounded up in the villages of [Imwas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imwas), [Yalo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yalo) and [Bayt Nuba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bayt_Nuba) in the [Latrun Salient](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latrun_Salient) and ordered by the Israeli military into exile eastwards. All three villages were then destroyed, and within two years the area was planned as a recreational area now called [Canada Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada_Park).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKelly2004100–101-56] Tens of thousands of Palestinians fled to Jordan from the refugee camps of [Aqabat Jaber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aqabat_Jaber) and [Ein as-Sultan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ein_as-Sultan_camp) after Israel bombed the camps.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBlack2017-57] The overall numbers of Palestinians displaced by that war is generally estimated to have been around 280,000–325,000, of which it has been calculated that some 120–170,000 were two-time refugees, [having been displaced earlier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Palestinian_exodus) during the [1948 war](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1947%E2%80%931949_Palestine_war).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBowker200381,_n.6-58] The number who left the West Bank as a consequence of the war ranges from 100,000 to 400,000,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenistiZamir1995299,_n._18-59] of which from 50,000 to 200,000 lived in the Jordan Valley.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHareuveni20118-60]

### Military-civil administration

Main articles: [Israeli Military Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Military_Governorate) and [Israeli Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration)

During the 1967 war, Israel appointed a Military Governor to rule the West Bank, with a remit to retain [Jordanian law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_Jordan) except where these conflicted with Israel's rights as [belligerent occupying power](816214.md). The Israeli administration of Palestinian territories became in time "the longest – and, accordingly, the most entrenched and institutionalized – belligerent occupation in modern history",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBen-NaftaliSfardViterbo20183-61] issuing from 1967 to 2014 over 1,680 military orders regarding the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrancis2014391-62] The third military order, issued two days after the onset of the occupation, specified that military courts were to apply the provisions of the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention) regarding the protection of civilians in a war zone: within 4 months this stipulation was erased from the order.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShehadeh2020-63] Jordan maintains that some of the laws ostensibly retained from its code, stemming from the Mandatory [Defence (Emergency) Regulations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Defence_\(Emergency\)_Regulations) of 1945, had in fact been abolished, and were invalid as they conflicted with the Fourth Geneva Convention of 1949. The [Israeli Military Governorate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Military_Governorate) was dissolved in 1981, and in its place the Israeli military established the [Israeli Civil Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Civil_Administration). The military order establishing the Israeli Civil Administration, military order 947, specified that "the Civil Administration shall administer civilian affairs ... with regard to the welfare and benefit of the population."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlayfair1988411-64][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198463-65][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEU2012221-66][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKanonich20175–6-67] [Meron Benvenisti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meron_Benvenisti) argues that this transition marked the transformation of the occupation from a temporary into a permanent system.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWeinthalSowers2019325-68]

The military closely supervised elections in local clubs, cooperatives, or charitable organizations. West Bank lawyers were banned on security grounds from organizing a [bar association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bar_association).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlayfair1988409–410-69] Palestinians were denied direct political representation after 1976, and instead, [village leagues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Village_Leagues) (*rawabit al-qura*) were introduced,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198462-70][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlayfair1988409-71] and furnished by Israel with arms and militias. These leagues had a short life: their appointees were considered to be [quislings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quisling) by General [Binyamin Ben-Eliezer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Binyamin_Ben-Eliezer)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTETessler1994553-72] and collaborationists by the local population, and to have been recruited from people who were lazy or had criminal backgrounds.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198462-70][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBisharat201266–67-73] With the [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), Israel negotiated with the [Palestine Liberation Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) a provisory agreement which left the latter some autonomy in Area A, mixed regulation of Area B, and total Israeli administration of the largest zone, Area C. Israel retains a right to operate militarily in all three zones,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESimpson2001101-74] but security issues have a bilateral dimension that had led a number of critics to argue that effectively the Palestinian National Authority has become Israel's subcontractor in the occupation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBeinart2014-75] According to an analysis by the Israeli think tank [Molad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Molad:_The_Center_for_the_Renewal_of_Israeli_Democracy) in 2017, Israel deploys 50–75% of its active [IDF](39237.md) forces in the West Bank,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordisLevi20176,_15-76] while only one-third deals with Arab states, [Iran](14653.md), [Hezbollah](13919.md), [Hamas](13913.md) and other perceived external threats.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKadmon2016-77] 80% of the former defend settlements, while 20% handle any behavior that Israel considers a security threat, including terrorism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordisLevi20176,_15-76]

### Israeli security concerns

A concern for security in Israel has been said to "vastly exceed the norm for other Western countries".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMintz1983626-78] Israel's [military-industrial sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military%E2%80%93industrial_complex), which by the early 1980s employed a quarter of all industrial workers with 28% of GNP devoted to defense expenditures, became the fastest growing sector of the economy after 1967.[^cite_note-79] It came to extend its activities beyond issues of defense, spilling over into the administration and settlement of the occupied territories.[^cite_note-80]

The occupation has, according to some Israeli researchers, produced an ethos of conflict of which perceived security concerns, at times perplexing to outsiders,[^cite_note-81] are a central feature.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBar-TalSalomon200624ff-82][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBar-TalAlon2017317–318-83] Although security is a fundamental Israeli preoccupation, the state has never formalized an official national security policy or doctrine.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreilich20186–7-84][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThrall2017100–101-85] Before June 1967, the Israeli cabinet did not regard the West Bank as having a "vital security value".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorenberg200749-86] Before the war ended, the [IDF](39237.md)'s research department under [Shlomo Gazit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shlomo_Gazit) drew up a proposal to pull back from the West Bank and Gaza almost completely in exchange for a peace treaty, since, they concluded, there was no need for retaining any territory on security grounds. The document was ignored.[^cite_note-87] It was in the wake of the conquest that secure, defensible borders became a keynote of Israel's foreign policy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInbar200792-88]

Four schools of thought came to dominate the question of the acquired territories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELukacs19996–14-89] Two were closely linked to strategic questions of security. The *territorialist* approach, associated with [Yigal Allon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yigal_Allon)'s [Allon Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allon_Plan) (1967–1970), would have annexed all of the West Bank south of Jerusalem and the lowland border along the [Jordan River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_River), and excluded areas closer to the pre-1967 border, which had a high density of Palestinians. The *functionalist* view, associated with [Moshe Dayan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Dayan) and later [Simon Peres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Peres), foresaw setting up five army bases along the Jordan ridges, which also left the Palestinians in between with a degree of autonomy, though constrained to accept the presence of Israelis among them.[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91][^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200212–13-92] From 1968 to 1977 [Labor governments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party) facilitated a number of settlements designed to form a bulwark against the threat of future mass tank assaults from Jordan and Iraq.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2009206-93]

The third approach, associated with [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin) and the [Likud party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud), is *annexationist*, and with the ascendency of Likud, the biblical resonance of West Bank territory outweighed questions of security significance in driving an expansion of settlements,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2009206-93] though both Likud and [Gush Emunim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Emunim) came to oppose Palestinian independence on security grounds and treated West Bank Palestinians as either potential enemies or security threats, by arguing that national autonomy would develop into a basis for PLO aggression.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaoz2015163–164-94] The fourth position, associated with [Abba Eban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abba_Eban), [Pinhas Sapir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinhas_Sapir) and [Yehoshafat Harkabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yehoshafat_Harkabi) is *reconciliationist*, being opposed to the idea of "Fortress Israel". Its proponents do not generally consider retention of the West Bank indispensable for guaranteeing Israel's security interests, with Harkabi, a former head of military intelligence, advocating withdrawal to the 1967 borders in exchange for a negotiated settlement with the PLO.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELukacs199914-95]

The West Bank was considered a bargaining chip in securing a broad peace treaty with Arab nations.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaoz201512-96] In time, especially after the [Sinai withdrawal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Egypt%E2%80%93Israel_peace_treaty), and suggestions the [Golan Heights](7515865.md) were also negotiable, the idea of retaining territory for strategic interests dwindled in importance, as a military anachronism in an age of missile warfare.[^cite_note-97] The military arguments for retaining ground were supplanted by political considerations, that Arab acquiescence in agreed on borders is of greater importance, and that settlements, formerly placed along possible invasion routes, were no longer functional for security, if they were an obstacle to peace.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEInbar200792-88] The [Oslo Accords](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oslo_Accords), moreover, had set in place a Palestinian security apparatus that, as [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin) acknowledged, worked with Israel to safeguard Israel's security interests.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon2008189-98]

According to analysts who support Israeli settlements, the presence of hostile, armed forces on the high ground of the West Bank would pose a security risk to the narrow Israeli land between the West Bank and the Mediterranean coast, which contains some of the country's biggest strategic assets, including Ben Gurion airport, the largest electric power station, and highly populated cities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKohaviNowiak2017158,_166-99] Over half of the Israeli public believes settlements reinforce Israel's security. In recent years, numerous top defense experts disagree, dismissing the idea as a myth or outdated illusion.[^cite_note-100][^cite_note-101] 106 retired Israeli generals, such as [Eyal Ben-Reuven](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyal_Ben-Reuven), [Moshe Kaplinsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Kaplinsky) and [Gadi Shamni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gadi_Shamni),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHarel2017-102] and [Shin Bet](311752.md) heads, such as [Yuval Diskin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuval_Diskin),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESherwood2013-103] have publicly opposed [Benjamin Netanyahu](56472.md)'s claim that an independent state of Palestine would be a security threat, arguing variously that holding millions of Palestinians under occupation on ostensible security-related grounds, rather than pursuing [an overall peace plan with Arab countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arab_Peace_Initiative), endangers Israel's future.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoldberg2014-104]

## Territory

Further information: [West Bank areas in the Oslo II Accord](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_areas_in_the_Oslo_II_Accord) and [East Jerusalem](343508.md)

Israel extended its jurisdiction over East Jerusalem on 28 June 1967, suggesting internally it was annexed while maintaining abroad that it was simply an administrative move to provide services to residents.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenistiZamir1995307-105][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKarayanni20144-106] The move was deemed "null and void" by the [United Nations Security Council](31956.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenisti2012205–206-107][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDumper2010119-108] The elected Arab council was disbanded, and a number of services provided by Palestinian companies were transferred to their Israeli competitors. The population ratio for this united Jerusalem was set ideally as 76% Jewish and 24% Arab,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMalki200025–26-109] and Jewish Israeli settlers were given a 5-year tax exemption, not applied to Palestinian Jerusalemites, who were placed in a high income tax bracket, and paid for 26% of municipal services while receiving 5% of the benefits.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMalki200034-110] The Palestinian areas were encircled by [Jewish new town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_town) developments which effectively closed them off from expansion, and services to the latter were kept low so that after decades, basic infrastructure was left in neglect, with shortages of schools, inadequate sewage and garbage disposal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECheshinHutmanMelamed200921-111] By 2017, 370,000 lived in the overcrowded Arab areas, living under severe restrictions on their daily movement and commerce.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem2017a-112] One 2012 report stated that the effect of Israeli policies was that, amidst flourishing modern Jewish settlements, the Arab sector had been allowed to decay into a slum where criminals, many of them collaborators, thrived.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEICG2012i–ii,_1-113] In 2018 legislative measures were announced to strip a further 12,000 Palestinians of their right to live in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELazaroff2018-114]

Israel's policies regarding the use of land in the rest of the West Bank display three interlocking aspects, all designed around a project of [Judaization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaization_of_Jerusalem) of what was Palestinian territory. These policies consist of (a) planning for land use, (b) expropriations of land, and (c) the construction of settlements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAbdulhadi199046-115]

### Area C

Main article: [Area C (West Bank)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C_\(West_Bank\))



&#x20; Area C

&#x20; East Jerusalem

The "Letters of Mutual Recognition" accompanying the "Israel-PLO Declaration of Principles on Interim Self-Government Arrangements" (the DOP), signed in Washington on 13 September 1993, provided for a transitional period not exceeding five years of Palestinian interim self-government in the Gaza Strip and the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERivlin2010159-116] Major critics of these arrangements, headed by [Raja Shehadeh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raja_Shehadeh), argue that the [PLO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestine_Liberation_Organization) had scarce interest or competence in the legal implications of what it was signing.[^cite_note-117]

These Oslo Accords ceded nominal control of a small amount of the West Bank to a [Palestinian authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Authority_government_in_the_West_Bank), with a provisory division of the land, excluding East Jerusalem, into 3 areas: Area A (18% of territory, 55% of population), Area B (20% of territory, 41% of the population), and [Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C_\(West_Bank\)) (62% of territory, 5.8% of population). Israel never finalized the undertaking with regard to Area C to transfer zoning and planning from the Israeli to the Palestinian authorities within five years and all administrative functions continued to remain in its hands.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEU2012221-66] Tactically, the Accord lessened Israel's problem with large-scale demonstrations since the areas of ostensible PA control were fragmented into 165 islands containing 90% of the Palestinian population, all surrounded by the spatially contiguous 60% of the West Bank where the PA was forbidden to venture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEThrall2017144-118][^cite_note-121] Israel then reasserted in 2000 a right to enter, according to "operational needs", Area A where most West Bank Palestinians live and which is formally under PA administration, meaning they still effectively control all the West Bank including areas under nominal PA authority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEToI2016-122][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenisti2012211-123][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZiai2013130-124]

According to the [United Nations special rapporteur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_special_rapporteur) on Human Rights in the Palestinian Territories, [Michael Lynk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Lynk), the policies applied by Israel indicate an intention to annex totally Area C,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELazaroff2018-114] which has [86% of the nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_parks_and_nature_reserves_of_Israel#National_parks_in_occupied_territories), 91% of the forests, 48% of the wells and [37% of the springs in the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_expropriation_of_Palestinian_springs_in_the_West_Bank).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWorld_Bank20136-125]

## Early economic impact of occupation

The early occupation set severe limits on public investment and comprehensive development programmes in the territories. British and Arab commercial banks operating in the West Bank were closed down soon after Israel assumed power there. [Bank Leumi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_Leumi) then opened nine branches, without successfully replacing the earlier system. Farmers could get loans, but Palestinian businessmen avoided taking out loans from them since they charged 9% compared to 5% interest in Jordan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen1985245-126][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Arkadie1977112–113-127] Land confiscations led to rural labour seeking employment, even if mainly menial, in Israel, causing a labour scarcity in the West Bank, and their remittances were the major factor in Palestinian economic growth during the 1969–73 boom years.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Arkadie1977110-128]

The Israeli licensing system stipulated no industrial plant could be built without obtaining a prior Israeli permit, which was often tied to security concerns. Entrepreneurs were denied a permit for a cement factory in Hebron, melon production was forbidden, imports of grapes and dates banned to protect Israeli farmers, and limits were set to how many cucumbers and tomatoes could be produced.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEQuigley2005186-129] Israeli milk producers exerted pressure on the Ministry for Industry and Trade to stop the establishment of a competitive dairy in [Ramallah](44132.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEl-FarraMacMillen2000161–161-130] [Ian Lustick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ian_Lustick) states that Israel "virtually prevented" Palestinian investment in local industry and agriculture.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELustick201811-131] Two decades later, 90% of West Bank imports came from Israel, with consumers paying more than they would for comparable products had they been able to exercise commercial autonomy.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKadri1998517–518-132]

### Land seizure mechanisms

Main article: [Land expropriation in the West Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_expropriation_in_the_West_Bank)

In 1968, a military order stopped attempts by Palestinians to register their land, while permitting Israel to register areas as state land with its own [Custodian of Enemy Property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Custodian_of_Enemy_Property).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200254-133] Whereas Ottoman and British Mandatory Authorities had used property-tax books to collect taxes from villages, Israel ignored these as evidence of ownership, demanding instead that proof be given that the land was under cultivation, while army seizures often prevented villagers from continuing to work their fields.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShezaf2021-134] From 1967 to 1983, Israel expropriated over 52% of the West Bank, most of its prime agricultural land, and, by the eve of the 1993 Oslo Accords, these confiscations had encompassed over three-quarters of the territory.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKadri1998517–518-132] The mechanisms by which Israel seizes or expropriates West Bank land were set forth in a detailed work by [B'Tselem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B'Tselem) in 2002.[^cite_note-135] Many practices outlined there were confirmed in the official Israeli [Sasson Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sasson_Report) of 2005, which focused on government subsidies and support for the creation of illegal [Israeli outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost) in knowing contravention of Israel's own laws.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalnoor2010138–139-136][^cite_note-137]

Under international law, a military may take temporary possession of occupied land, but not expropriate it. From 1957 to 1976 the IDF repeatedly requisitioned private Palestinian properties on the grounds of military necessity, only to turn them over for Jewish settlements, such as [Matitiyahu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matityahu,_Mateh_Binyamin), [Neve Tzuf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neve_Tzuf), [Rimonim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rimonim), [Bet El](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bet_El), [Kokhav Hashahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kokhav_Hashahar), [Alon Shvut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alon_Shvut), [El'azar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El'azar), [Efrat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Efrat_\(Israeli_settlement\)), [Har Gilo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Har_Gilo), [Migdal Oz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Migdal_Oz), [Gittit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gitit_\(moshav\)), [Yitav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitav) and [Qiryat Arba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qiryat_Arba). This practice, after Palestinians appealed, was blocked by the High Court in the case of [Elon Moreh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elon_Moreh) (1979).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200248-138] Thereafter, the [Ottoman Land Law of 1858](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_Land_Law_of_1858), which enabled the sovereign to seize certain types of land, though much private land had not been registered to avoid taxes or military service with the Ottomans, was reactivated.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200253–54-139] Thirdly, land temporally abandoned during the 1967 war deemed [absentee property](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_land_and_property_laws) came under [trusteeship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trust_law), but since Israel rarely allows refugees to return, instances where land is restored to its rightful owners are few and far between. If a claim is made, but the Custodian has sold it to a settler group in the meantime, the sale cannot be nullified even if invalid.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200258–59-140] Fourthly, land expropriated for public need under Jordanian law required notification, time for appeal, and royal approval. Israel modified this by delegating the power to regional military commanders, and by abolishing the requirement to publish the intention to expropriate in an [official gazette](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_gazette). Appeals were no longer dealt with in local courts but by the military court system.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200260-141] Lastly, land sales were subject to severe restrictions, except for purchases by the [Jewish National Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_National_Fund). Since Palestinians regard the sale of their land to Jews as treasonable, the law was altered to enable Jewish buyers to withhold registration of property acquired from Palestinians for 15 years. Many fraudulent practices in this regard flourished until they were formally stopped by law in 1985.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200262–63-142]

One estimate put the amount of [unalienable Islamic property dedicated to pious ends](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waqf) confiscated by Israel at over 600,000 dunams.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMusaeeAbbasMujanSidik201425,_36-143]

## Settlement

Main articles: [Israeli settlement](15123.md) and [List of Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_settlements)



On the left, the [Shu'fat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shu'fat_Camp) Palestinian refugee camp, bordered by the [Israeli West Bank wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Bank_barrier), facing the Israeli settlement of [Pisgat Ze'ev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pisgat_Ze'ev). East Jerusalem, 2023

[Ariel Sharon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ariel_Sharon) viewed the primary function of settling the West Bank as one of precluding the possibility of the formation of a Palestinian state, and his aim in promoting the [1982 invasion of Lebanon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_invasion_of_Lebanon) was to secure perpetual control of the former.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKimmerling200380–82-144][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShlaim2015344–245-145] As of 2017, excluding East Jerusalem, 382,916 Israelis have settled in the West Bank, and 40% (approximately 170,000 in 106 other settlements) live outside the major settlement blocs, where 214,000 reside.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerger2017-146]



Israeli settlement of [Carmel, Har Hebron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carmel,_Har_Hebron)



[Umm al-Khair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Umm_al-Khair,_Hebron) Palestinian village near Carmel

A continuity has often been observed between the [Realpolitik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realpolitik)[^cite_note-147] processes governing the creation of Israel and the practices adopted with regard to the West Bank.[^cite_note-148][^cite_note-149] Several analysts have likened the process to [enclosure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclosure) – the "establishment of exclusionary Jewish spaces on the Palestinian landscape" being heir to the English appropriation of [common land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_land) and its conversion to private use – or to the conversion of [Amerindian land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas) into "white property".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFields20175-150][^cite_note-151]

Early Zionist policy for land appropriation was outlined by [Menachem Ussishkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Ussishkin)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPenslar200734–35-152] in 1904 and, aside from voluntary sales, also foresaw the need to seize land by war and compel sale through expropriation via the ruling authority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPoole200785-153] It called this practice "colonization", a word which, since 1967, has been replaced by the [euphemism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euphemism)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMerip197714-154] "settlement".[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVeracini201326–42-156]

The technique developed over the decades of early settlement was one of incremental spread, setting up tower-and-stockade outposts, a pattern repeated in the West Bank after 1967.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECook2013a5-157] A quote attributed to [Joseph Trumpeldor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Trumpeldor) summed up Zionist logic: "Wherever the Jewish plow plows its last furrow, that is where the border will run".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordisLevi20177-158] The principle of this slow steady establishing of "facts on the ground" before the adversary realizes what is going on, is colloquially known as "dunam after dunam, goat after goat".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEChomskyAchcarShalom2015179-159] The model applied to the West Bank was that used for the [Judaization of the Galilee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaization_of_the_Galilee), consisting of setting up a checkered pattern of settlements not only around Palestinian villages but in between them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198461–62-160] In addition to settlements considered legal, with government sponsorship, there are some 90 [Israeli outposts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_outpost) (2013) built by private settler initiatives which, though illegal even in Israeli terms, are defended by the IDF.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordisLevi201716-161] From the mid-1990s to 2015 many of these, such as [Amona](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amona,_Mateh_Binyamin), [Avri Ran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avri_Ran)'s [Giv'ot Olam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giv'ot_Olam) and [Ma'ale Rehav'am](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ale_Rehav'am) – the latter on 50 dunams of private Palestinian land – were directly funded, according to [Haaretz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haaretz), by loans from the [World Zionist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Zionist_Organization) through Israeli taxpayer money,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerger2018-162] since its approximate \$140 million income derives from Israel and is mostly invested in settlements in the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAnthonyEgalRiesBond201517-163]

The first site chosen for settlement was [Gush Etzion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Etzion), on some 75 acres (30 ha) worked by Palestinian refugees.[^cite_note-164] [Hanan Porat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hanan_Porat) was inspirational, intending by developing the settlement in order to put in place a practical application of the [radical messianic Zionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_Zionism) of Rabbi [Zvi Yehuda Kook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zvi_Yehuda_Kook),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorenberg2007103,_105–106-165][^cite_note-166] whose father [Abraham Isaac Kook](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abraham_Isaac_Kook)'s [Mercaz HaRav](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mercaz_HaRav) [yeshiva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yeshiva) in particular has exercised considerable influence on Israel's policies regarding the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShafir1984815–816-167][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchnellMishal2008247–248-168] According to [Eyal Benvenisti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyal_Benvenisti), a 1972 judgement by Supreme Court justice [Moshe Landau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moshe_Landau), siding with a military commander's decision to assign electrical supply in the Hebron area to the [Israel Electric Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Electric_Corporation) rather than to a Palestinian company, was to prove pivotal to encouraging the settlement project, since it placed the latter under the jurisdiction of the military authorities.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenisti2012221–222-169]

During the first decade of Israel occupation, when the [Israeli Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_Labor_Party) held power, settlement was concentrated on constructing a ring of "residential fortresses" around the Palestinian population of Jerusalem, and in the [Jordan Valley](12105764.md). According to Ibrahim Matar, the purpose of this colonizing strategy around Jerusalem was to hem in and block the expansion of the Palestinian population, and to incentivize Palestinian emigration by inducing a sense among the Palestinians of living in a ghetto.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMatar198193–94-170]

Between 1967 and 1977, settlement was small-scale[^cite_note-171] totaling the transfer of 3,200 Israelis into the West Bank. By the end of Labor's term of power in 1977, 4,500 Israelis had established themselves in 30 West Bank settlements and some 50,000 in settlements in East Jerusalem.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELeinWeizman200212-172] It was with the rise to power of [Menachem Begin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menachem_Begin)'s [Likud Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likud), driven by a "Greater Israel theology" that year, which led to an incremental expansion of this projects,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalchinsky2004117-173] and marked in the view of [Oren Yiftachel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oren_Yiftachel) the peak of Israel's [ethnocratic project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnocracy), with the West Bank to become "the bedrock of Jewish national identity".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYiftachel200665-174] A change in territorial focus took place, with settlements now promoted in the biblical heartland of the West Bank next to Palestinian population centres.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERivlin2010143-175] The main plank of Likud's platform, still unaltered, called for the immediate annexation of the West Bank.[^cite_note-176] If security calculations influenced the relatively small-scale settlements advanced by the Israeli Labor Party, the reconfirmation of Likud in 1981 led to a rapid escalation of settlement as a religious-national programme.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESelby2003b76-177]

At the same time the military censors forbade the local Palestinian press from reporting any news about settlements, expropriations or legal moves made to block them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFriedman198399-178] By 1983, settlers in the West Bank numbered 28,400.[^cite_note-179] Incentives consisting of government mortgage and housing subsidies, tax incentives, business grants, free schooling, infrastructure projects, and defense were provided. After the Oslo Accords down to 2002, the settler population doubled.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalchinsky2004117-173]

In 1972 the number of Israeli settlers in Area C were 1,200, in 1993 110,000, and in 2010 310,000 (excluding East Jerusalem). Before 1967 there were between 200,000 and 320,000 Palestinians in the Jordan Valley,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEU2012220-180] which, together with the northern Dead Sea, covers 30% of the West Bank and constituted the "most significant land reserve" for Palestinians, 85% of whom are barred from entering it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem2017c-181] By 2011, 37 settlements had been established among the 64,451 Palestinians there (who constitute 29 communities)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHareuveni20118-60] 70% of whom live in Area A in Jericho.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEU2012220-180] According to [ARIJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Applied_Research_Institute%E2%80%93Jerusalem), by 2015 only 3 of 291 Palestinian communities in Area C received Israeli building approval (on just 5.7 hectares), and any construction outside that was subject to demolition. In that one year, they calculate, Israel confiscated a further 41,509 hectares, demolished 482 homes – displacing 2,450 people – uprooted 13,000 trees, and subjected Palestinians and their property to assault on some 898 distinct occasions. Israeli settlements constituted 6% of the land, while military zones had been declared over 29%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEARIJ20164–5,_12-182]

From 1967 to 2003, successive Israeli governments assisted the transfer of some 230,000 Jewish civilians into 145 West Bank and Gaza settlements and approximately 110 outposts.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalchinsky2004116-183] By 2016, approximately 42% of the settlement workforce (55,440) found employment in those settlements.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHRW2016-184] The ultra-Orthodox have dominated the process from the beginning: from 2003 to 2007 alone the population of [Beitar Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beitar_Illit), whose construction was facilitated by the expropriation of 1,500 dunams of [Naḥḥālīn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahalin) farmland,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFields2017173,_300-185] rose 40%, while [Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit), built on the Palestinian village lands of [Ni'lin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ni'lin), [Kharbata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kharbata), [Saffa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saffa,_Ramallah), [Bil'in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bil'in) and [Dir Qadis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dir_Qadis),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKing2009149-186][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShafir201775-187] increased by 55%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERivlin2010148-188]



[Modi'in Illit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modi'in_Illit), the biggest Israeli settlement of the occupied West Bank ([Area C](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_C)), in 2012

The majority of Israeli West Bank agriculture arises from contracts with the [World Zionist Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Zionist_Organization) that bypass direct contracts with the Israeli Land Regulating Commissioner, and many were given to use private Palestinian land.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHRW2016-184] With the [Regularization Law of 2017](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regulation_Law), Israel retroactively legalized the settler takeover of thousands of hectares of privately owned Palestinian land and some 4,500 homes which settlers had built without obtaining official permits.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAI2018b207-189] By that year, the fifth decade of occupation, Israel had managed to establish (2017) 237 settlements, housing roughly 580,000 settlers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHRW2017a-190]

One technique used to established settlements was to set up a [paramilitary encampment for army personnel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nahal_settlement) to be used for agricultural and military training for soldiers. These were then slowly transformed into civilian settlements,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMerip197715-191] often without official approval.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHareuveni20118-60] This could be justified as legal because they were initially IDF bases without civilians.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeleg1995161–162,_n.12-192] Another technique was to render land momentarily unusable. [Gitit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gitit,_Bik'at_HaYarden) for example was established by closing off 5,000 dunams of the village lands of [Aqraba](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aqraba,_Nablus) and then spraying it with defoliants.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMerip197718-193]

On occasion, creating settlements is hailed as a measure to punish Palestinians collectively, as a reaction to a Palestinian killing of a settler, or in response to the granting of non-member observer status to the [Palestinian State](241405.md) by the United Nations, an announcement which generated plans for a further 3,000 settler homes in the West Bank.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFisk2018-194] Economic motivations also drive settlement: selling a 50–60 sq. m. apartment in Jerusalem allows the purchase of an apartment three times larger in settlements like [Ma’aleh Adumim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma%E2%80%99aleh_Adumim).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerger2017-146] One early metaphor likened the expansion of settlements to the [baobab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baobab) tree in [The Little Prince](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Little_Prince), whose seeds take root and eventually cover the entire planet. By the early eighties, several authoritative observers, among them [Eyal Benvenisti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyal_Benvenisti), already concluded that the settlement expansion was close to a point of no return from total annexation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELustick201813-195] The impression left of the landscape has been described as follows:

> Israeli settlements form an upper-middle-class oasis of green grass, shopping malls, and swimming pools amidst open desert and enclaves of Palestinian refugee camps, villages, and towns with limited access to water.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGautney200972-196][^cite_note-197]

American citizens lead the [diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diaspora) in moving into West Bank settlements, with 12% stating their first choice of residency is "Judea and Samaria". They now form the predominant block and number an estimated 60,000.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHirschhorn2017-198][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMaltz2017-199]

### Legal status

Main article: [International law and Israeli settlements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_law_and_Israeli_settlements)

Before proceeding with settlement, the government sought legal advice from their resident expert on international law, [Theodor Meron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theodor_Meron).[^cite_note-200] His top secret memorandum stated unequivocally that the prohibition on any such [population transfer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_transfer) was categorical, and that "civilian settlement in the administered territories contravenes the explicit provisions of the [Fourth Geneva Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fourth_Geneva_Convention)",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorenberg200799–100-201] indicating that the Prime Minister [Levi Eshkol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Levi_Eshkol) was therefore aware the promotion of settlements in the West Bank would be illegal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGorenberg2007100-202] The International community has also since rejected Israel's unwillingness to accept the applicability of the Geneva Conventions to the territories it occupies,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenistiZamir1995305–306-203][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPoissonnierDavid2020-204] with most arguing all states are duty bound to observe them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalchinsky2004117-173] Israel alone challenges this premise, arguing that the West Bank and Gaza are "disputed territories",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFalk200222-205] and that the Conventions do not apply because these lands did not form part of another state's sovereign territory, and that the transfer of Jews into areas like the West Bank is not a government act but a voluntary movement by Israeli Jewish people.[^cite_note-207]

The [International Court of Justice](14918.md) also determined that Israeli settlements in the West Bank were established in breach of international law in their 2004 [advisory opinion on the West Bank barrier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israeli_West_Bank_barrier#International_Court_of_Justice).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEICJ2004-208] In 1980, Israel declined to sign the [Vienna Convention on the Law of Treaties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna_Convention_on_the_Law_of_Treaties) which obliges national laws to give way to international law when the two conflict, and regulates settlements in terms of its own laws, in lieu of any compulsion to observe its treaty commitments and by arguing that all the relevant UN bodies adjudicating the matter are "[anti-Zionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Zionism) and [anti-Semitic](1078.md)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalchinsky2004119-209][^cite_note-210]

### Settler violence

Main articles: [Israeli settler violence](47246246.md) and [Price tag policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_tag_policy)



Fences to protect Palestinians in the old town of [Hebron](38577.md) from the garbage thrown by Israeli settlers on the upper floors, 2010



"Fight the enemy. [Price Tag](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Price_tag_policy)." Hebrew Graffiti spray-painted by Israeli settlers in [Urif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urif)



"No more administrative orders." Graffiti spray-painted in Hebrew by [Israeli settlers](15123.md) on a car in [Fara'ata](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fara'ata), 2018

Though settler [vigilantism](58325.md) dates back to the late 1970s, when they were authorized to bear arms in self-defense – one ordinance exempted them from military service in Israel while drafting them into West Bank units and another gave them powers to demand Palestinians provide identification and even to arrest them[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBisharat201254–65-211] – settler terrorism formally dates back at least to the [Jewish Underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Underground) movement of the early 1980s, which began by targeting and severely maiming, through the deployment of [car bombs](155282.md), West Bank mayors such as [Bassam Shakaa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bassam_Shakaa) of [Nablus](233369.md), and [Karim Khalaf](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karim_Khalaf) of [Ramallah](44132.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGazit200394–99-212][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBymanSachs201275-213] In the first two years of the [First Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Intifada), settlers killed at least 34 Palestinians, four below the age of 16, with 11 killed by settler initiative at home or while guarding flocks; a further six probably died through settler actions, and eight were killed in response to stone throwing at cars. Only two died as a result of clashes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStockton199094-214] In the 1980s attempts by one Jewish terrorist group led by [Meir Kahane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meir_Kahane) to set up settlements were blocked by other settlers, the heads of [Gush Emunim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gush_Emunim), though Kahane's views would later motivate the [Cave of the Patriarchs Massacre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cave_of_the_Patriarchs_Massacre) in Hebron.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPedahzurPerliger200973,_70–74-215]

From 2009 such settler violence escalated rapidly, an uptick that coincided with a dramatic fall in Palestinian terror attacks. In 2009, 200 settler attacks took place, a figure which doubled to over 400 by 2011. Of the latter, nearly 300 consisted in attacks on Palestinian property, causing 100 Palestinian casualties, and the destruction of roughly 10,000 trees.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBymanSachs201275-213] Many of these are carried out as [Price tag acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Israeli_price_tag_attacks),[^cite_note-216] which target innocent Palestinians and are designed to intimidate the local population. [Yesh Din](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yesh_Din) discovered that of 781 such incidents covered from 2005 to 2011, 90% of the Israeli investigations were closed without indictments, and many of the culprits were [Hilltop Youth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hilltop_Youth).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBymanSachs201276-217] In an analysis of 119 cases of settlers killing Palestinians, it emerged that only 13 were sent to jail: six were convicted of murder, only one of whom was sentenced to life imprisonment, while of seven convicted of manslaughter, one received a prison sentence of seven and a half years for killing a child, and the rest were given light sentences.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon2008144-218]

Writing in 2012, [Daniel Byman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daniel_Byman) and Natan Sachs judged that the pattern of settler violence was "undoubtedly working" and achieving its ends, by influencing the way Palestinians view Israelis, strengthening the hand of terrorists among them, and by seeding fears in the Israeli government that any pullout in exchange for peace would lead to conflict with settlers and a political disaster for the political parties involved.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBymanSachs201282–83-219]

## State of asymmetric war



Israeli soldiers patrolling [Awarta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Awarta).

West Bank Palestinians have engaged in two uprisings that have led to an [asymmetric set of wars of attrition](46863.md), between the occupying power and the occupied people.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECordesman2006268-220][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalloMarzano20091–18-221][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECohen2010a99–111-222] This characterization has been further refined by classifying the conflict as structurally asymmetric, where the root cause of tension lies in the standoff between a colonizer and the colonized, and in which the large power imbalance in favour of the dominator leads to a resort to [guerilla tactics or terrorism](12720.md) by the dominated.[^cite_note-223] Much of what Palestinians defend as acts of "resistance" are, in Israeli usage, regarded as "terrorism".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHunt201345-224] Making speeches calling on fellow Palestinians to resist the occupation is construed in Israeli law as tantamount to advocacy of terrorism. In the case of the parliamentarian [Azmi Bishara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azmi_Bishara), the Knesset voted to lift his parliamentary immunity in order to pave the way for a criminal indictment on this charge.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFalk200219-225][^cite_note-226]

International law does not address the issue regarding the rights of an occupied people to resist an occupation which flagrantly violates fundamental human rights.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFalk200226-227] [The United Nations General Assembly Resolution 1514](6075560.md) established that force may not be used to deny self-determination, and that recourse to force to resist colonial or alien domination is legitimate.[^cite_note-228]

The two fundamental preconditions for containing conflict – clearly defined borders and rough power parity between the parties at war – are absent,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGrinberg20095-229] with a pronounced economic and military disparity favouring Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBar-Siman-Tov200719,_26-230] The disparity extends, according to [Nathan Thrall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Thrall), to the numerous negotiations over a peace settlement.[^cite_note-231] According to [Aharon Klieman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aharon_Klieman), even Israeli negotiating tactics with Palestinians follow the principles of warfare used by the IDF.[^cite_note-232]

### Armaments (Israel)

In terms of armaments, Israel is reputed to have "the strongest and best-equipped army of the Middle East."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalloMarzano20099-233] The arsenal at Israel's disposal to counteract major Palestinian uprisings ranges from [F-16 fighters](11642.md), [Merkava tanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merkava),[^cite_note-234] [Apache helicopters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apache_helicopter),[^cite_note-235] [Hellfire missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hellfire_missile), massive armoured [D9 Caterpillar bulldozers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Caterpillar_D9).[^cite_note-236] to the standard M-16 rifle[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBishara2010127–128-237] and the use of snipers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEB'Tselem2020-238]

The Israeli techniques for daily [dispersing protesting crowds](2349113.md) differ according to the ethnicity of the protestors. With Jewish settlers by and large the methods are those policing approaches used in Western countries, and they are reported as not intervening when settlers go on the rampage against Palestinians. With Palestinians, contrariwise, military tactics are adopted, and observers such as B'Tselem claim lack of proportionality and recourse to firearms is characteristic.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVan_Reenen2006382–386-239] With the latter at demonstrations Israeli forces have drawn on [rash gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CS_gas),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReinhart201196-240] tear gas canisters (which have often produced fatalities);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEStockton199093–94-241] shooting into crowds[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraff2015168–169-242] with [rubber-coated steel bullets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rubber_bullet), which can be lethal;[^cite_note-243] [high-velocity bullets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzzle_velocity);[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraff2015167-244] recourse to the use of live ammunition rounds; the deployment from 2008 of trucks dousing whole areas with [putrid Skunk spray](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skunk_\(weapon\));[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGleim2015-245] [stun grenades](662807.md); water cannons; pepper spray; [capsaisin projectiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capsaisin);[^cite_note-246] deployment of [snatch squads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Snatch_squad) and [mista'arvim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mista'arvim) and [sponge rounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plastic_bullet).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichaeli20137–46-247] The use of rubber-coated metal bullets is allowed in the West Bank but forbidden from deployment against people within Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichaeli201343-248] Also deployed on occasion since 2005 when they were used at [Bil’in](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bil%E2%80%99in), are [loud sound-wave generating devices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sonic_weapon),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichaeli201347-249] gravel-throwing machines;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraff2015167-244] shock-inducing [polystyrene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polystyrene) and [bismuth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bismuth) metal [paintball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paintball) pellets,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMichaeli201347–28-250] and [tasars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tasar). In the first Intifada, snipers targeted youths primarily to maim them, with dum dum shots to the right arm biceps crippling their use by stone-throwers for life.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAfflitto200750-251][^cite_note-252]

### Armaments (Palestinians)

The primary value developed by Palestinians to resist the occupation from 1967 has been [ṣumūd](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumud), hanging on stubbornly, a steadfast perseverance in remaining on one's land,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBraverman2009242-253][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESlyomovics199118-254] even if it turns into a prison,[^cite_note-255] in the face of Jewish *hitnahalut* (settlement).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYiftachel200665-174] The word itself was consistently repressed from Palestinian papers by Israeli censors in the early decades.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFriedman198398-256] [Mubarak Awad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mubarak_Awad), founder of the [Palestinian Centre for the Study of Nonviolence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_Centre_for_the_Study_of_Nonviolence), endeavoured to inculcate [Gandhian principles of non-violence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nonviolence) in the West Bank, and was subsequently expelled and sent into exile by Israel on the grounds that he was preaching non-violence only as a cover for an armed struggle for liberation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENusseibeh2015179–180-257] The village of Bil'in, one of the first villages, along with [Budrus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budrus) and [Abu Dis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abu_Dis), to practice Gandhian methods of non-violent resistance,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEShulman201834-258] has in one decade (2005–2015) been subjected to incessant night raids, seen hundreds of its residents arrested, its leader Abdullah Abu Rahmeh put on trial five times and sentenced to imprisonment, and thousands of demonstrators injured.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTERahmeh2015-259]

The mainstay of Palestinian armed resistance techniques to the occupation during the First Intifada, which was generally non-lethal,[^cite_note-260] consisted of [throwing stones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_stone-throwing) at Israeli troops during clashes, or at military and settler vehicles bearing their distinctive yellow number plates, together with tire-burning, hurling [Molotov cocktails](20922.md) and setting up roadblocks.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraff2015157ff-261] The then-[Defense Minister](3226446.md) [Yitzhak Rabin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yitzhak_Rabin)'s policy was that, "rioters must emerge with casualties or scars."[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraff2015170-262] The juxtaposition of this primitive method with Israeli power was striking, with children and youths throwing stones and deploying slingshots against a fully equipped and highly trained military power exerting overwhelming superiority.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGalloMarzano200910-263][^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265]

Years later, a spiral in escalation led to the increased use of knifing and [Palestinian suicide attacks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Palestinian_suicide_attacks) corresponding to the expansion of deployment of warplanes, helicopters, and recourse to assassinations by Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBar-Siman-Tov200720-266][^cite_note-267] In the [Al-Aqsa Intifada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Intifada), suicide bombers, [among which youths figured prominently](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_suicide_bombers_in_the_Israeli%E2%80%93Palestinian_conflict), were deployed and became a central feature from 2001 to 2005, of the second uprising.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlaw201663-268] Aside from the PLO's [Fatah](11104.md), many armed militant factions, Marxist, Islamic or otherwise, became involved, such as the [Tanzim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanzim), the [al-Aqsa Martyrs' Brigades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Aqsa_Martyrs'_Brigades), [Hamas](13913.md), [Islamic Jihad Movement in Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_Jihad_Movement_in_Palestine), the [Popular Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine), the [Democratic Front for the Liberation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Front_for_the_Liberation_of_Palestine), and [Popular Resistance Committees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_Resistance_Committees). This flared up into a large-scale military confrontation when, according to [Ma'ariv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ma'ariv), 700,000 rounds of ammunition were fired at West Bank crowds protesting the shooting of Palestinians in and around the [Haram al-Sharif](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haram_al-Sharif),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeled200648-269] killing 118 Palestinians, of whom 33 were teenagers.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeters201280-270][^cite_note-271] From 2001 to 2007 Israel killed more Palestinians annually than it had over the first two decades of occupation, averaging 674 as opposed to the earlier 32 per year.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon2008xvi–xvii-272] Kill ratios between the first and second intifada differ markedly. One Israeli was killed for every 25 Palestinians in the first, whereas the figure for the first year of the second the ratio varied from one Israeli to 2.5/3 Palestinians. The earlier ratio of 25\:1 was only reestablished by 2007.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPlaw201663–64-273][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUnispal2007-274]

The overall historic pattern of Palestinian violence in comparative terms, according to [Nathan Thrall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nathan_Thrall), appears to be far less participatory and deadly than other examples of local resistance to a foreign occupation. The four major outbreaks all began in civic demonstrations and strikes which, when violently repressed, led to a resort to violence.[^cite_note-275]

## Influence on other occupying regimes

In 1981, [Ya'akov Meridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ya'akov_Meridor) stated Israel aspired to play the role of "top proxy" for the United States in Central America.[^cite_note-276] By 1984, according to [Jan Nederveen Pieterse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Nederveen_Pieterse), Israel had become one of the world's major arms exporters, the largest arms supplier to Central America and Sub-Saharan Africa, and globally active in the business of counterinsurgency, expertise in which was gained in enforcing land expropriations and settlements in the West Bank, the Gaza Strip and the Galilee. Knowledge of this background, he argued,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198460,_64-277] was useful for assessing Israel's "export" of its methods, to countries like [Guatemala](17238567.md), [Honduras](13394.md), [Nicaragua](21362.md), [El Salvador](9356.md) and [Sri Lanka](26750.md), in some of which existed similar configurations of policy involving land, domination and exploitation, population policy, and terror.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPieterse198464,_67-278] One settlement project in [Costa Rica](5551.md) for example is thought to be based on Israeli expertise honed in West Bank projects.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBahbahButler1986158–159-279]

The Israeli techniques of [urban warfare](319027.md) against Palestinians in their towns and villages territories has influenced many other military powers.[^cite_note-280] Many argue that Israeli methods developed in their conflict with Palestinians have been a significant influence on U.S. military doctrines developed under the [administration of George W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_George_W._Bush).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham2010134,_136–138-281][^cite_note-FOOTNOTECook2013b16–17-282][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEZureik201023-283][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEDenes2010171–195-284][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGordon2010153–169-285] The American claim that new methods developed for the [war on terror](13425800.md) were necessary since the situation was unprecedented and therefore was a legal no man's land had a precedent in Israeli claims the war on terror in the West Bank was a legal [*terra nulla*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terra_nullius),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHajjar200632-286] and thus allowed approaches like extrajudicial and preemptive assassination,[^cite_note-287] a terminology already used by Israel with regard to its approach to resistance in the West Bank and Gaza. [Jeff Halper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jeff_Halper) has spoken of the risk of "Palestinizing" the American people as Israel has deepened its training programs for American police forces.[^cite_note-288][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKaplan2018-289][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERAIAJVP2018-290]

[Hoover Institution Fellow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hoover_Institution) and Senior Fellow at the [Joint Special Operations University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joint_Special_Operations_University) Thomas H. Henriksen writes that:

> The Israel Defense Forces' (IDF) military actions have been – and are – a crucible for methods, procedures, tactics, and techniques for the United States, which faces a similarly fanatical foe across the world in the Global War on Terror... Israeli experiences offer an historical record and a laboratory for tactics and techniques in waging counter-insurgencies or counterterrorist operations in America's Post-9/11 circumstances.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHenriksen20072-291][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham2010137-292]

## Legal status

Main article: [Legality of the Israeli occupation of Palestine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legality_of_the_Israeli_occupation_of_Palestine)

The legality of the occupation itself, while remaining much less covered than specific violations of international humanitarian law,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEImseis20201085-293][^cite_note-FOOTNOTESayed201480-294][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBen-NaftaliGrossMichaeli2005551–552-295] has drawn increasing attention from scholars and the international community, with multiple United Nations General Assembly resolutions describing the occupation itself as illegal. Scholars have largely concluded that, regardless of whether it was initially legal, the occupation has become illegal over time.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEImseis20201072–1073-296] Reasons cited for its illegality include use of force for impermissible purposes such as [annexation](18950961.md), violation of the Palestinian right to [self-determination](29269.md), that the occupation itself is an illegal regime "of alien subjugation, domination and exploitation", or some combination of these factors.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEImseis20201073,_1085-297] [Eyal Benvenisti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyal_Benvenisti) suggested that refusal by an occupier to engage in good faith with efforts to reach a peaceful solution should not only be considered illegal but as outright annexation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBenvenisti2012233-298] International law scholar [Ralph Wilde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralph_Wilde)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEUCL2017-299] states that "The common way of understanding the extended duration of the occupation... is a prolonged violation of international law".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWilde202143-300] However, Israel denies that it is occupying the territory and maintains that its presence is legal.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEICHR_report202331–36-301]

In 2022 a [United Nations fact-finding mission](69971286.md) released a report[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOHCHR_report2022-302] calling on the United Nations Security Council to end Israel's "permanent occupation" and for individual UN member states to prosecute Israeli officials. The report found "reasonable grounds" to conclude that the occupation "is now unlawful under international law due to its permanence" and Israel's "de-facto annexation policies".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOHCHR_finds_unlawful2022-303][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerman2022-304] Israeli prime minister [Yair Lapid](2448072.md) said the report is "biased, false, inciting and blatantly unbalanced" and tweeted that "Not all criticism of Israel is anti-Semitism, but this report was written by anti-Semites … and is a distinctly anti-Semitic report".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELazaroff2022-305] Later, the [International Court of Justice](14918.md) accepted a request to study [study the legality of the Israeli occupation](72240449.md)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEICoJ-306] and in 2024 concluded that Israel should put an end to its illegal occupation of the Palestinian territories.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBerg2024-307]

## Territorial fragmentation and domination over the Palestinians

Further information: [Palestinian enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_enclaves)

### Background

In 1920 [Israel Zangwill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel_Zangwill) argued that creating a state free of Jews would require a South African type of "racial redistribution".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeteet2016263-308] In 1931 [Arnold Toynbee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arnold_J._Toynbee) prophesied that, given the nature of the [Zionist project](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zionism) to secure land only for Jewish use to the exclusion of Palestinian labour, that the British mandatory government would be forced eventually to compensate the process by legislation that would create a [land reservation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palestinian_enclaves) for the exclusive use of Palestinians. He drew a parallel with the situation in South Africa under the [Natives Land Act, 1913](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natives_Land_Act,_1913) which established the principle of [segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation).[^cite_note-309] These segregated territorial reserves were the forerunners of the bantustans, a word that gained currency only much later in the 1940s. After the foundation of Israel in 1948, its first president [Chaim Weizmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaim_Weizmann) and South African Prime Minister [Jan Smuts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Smuts) supported each other's view on the [racial basis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnocracy) of their respective states and their rights over indigenous lands.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeteet2016262–263-310]

### Planning for fragmentation
